           Title: S. Thasci Caecili Cypriani Opera Omnia, Vol. III
                  Pars I (the genuine treatises: Ad Donatum, Quod
                  Idola Dii Non Sint, Ad Quirinum, De Habitu
                  Virginum, De Catholicae Ecclesiae Unitate, De
                  Lapsis, De Dominica Oratione, De Mortalitate, Ad
                  Fortunatum, Ad Demetrianum, De Opere et
                  Eleemosynis, De Bono Patientiae, De Zelo et Livore,
                  Sententiae Episcoporum)
      Creator(s): Cyprian of Carthage (Latin original, 3rd c. CE)
                  Guilelmus Hartel [Wilhelm Hartel] (Editor)
     Print Basis: Corpus Scriptorum Ecclesiasticorum Latinorum
                  (CSEL), Vol. III Pars I. Vindobonae [Vienna]: Apud
                  C. Geroldi Filium Bibliopolam Academiae, 1868.
          Rights: Public Domain
    Rights basis: 1868 publication date places this volume long out
                  of US copyright; Google Books scan (via archive.org,
                  item sthascicaecilic00hartgoog) carrying Google's own
                  public-domain finding for the item.
    Language: lat
        Supplied: pulled directly from archive.org by a sibling
                  research session with working network access
                  (session_01WLxhNbVhjkf1R2SAh8dxxT), 2026-09-08, for
                  the Latin Pastoral-Congregational Christianity world
                  build (not yet begun as a formal construction
                  thread) -- this world's own corpus map
                  (cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-
                  christianity.yaml) had assigned Cyprian's treatises
                  from the English ANF05 translation only; no
                  original-language witness existed in the vendored
                  corpus prior to this file.
    Content note: Verified this pass: title page reads "VOL. III.
                  PARS I," and the opening treatise "AD DONATVM" is
                  present and correctly headed. The remaining twelve
                  treatises listed above have not been individually
                  confirmed present this pass; per CSEL's own
                  well-documented contents for this Part, they are
                  expected to be, but a future session should verify
                  each specific treatise before citing it from this
                  file. This is Pars I (treatises) only -- Pars II
                  (Epistulae) is vendored as a separate file,
                  cyprian_epistulae-lat_hartel1871.txt; Pars III
                  (Opera Spuria) is a distinct volume, not vendored
                  this pass.


This is a digital copy of a book that was preserved for generations on library shelves before it was carefully scanned by Google as part of a project 
to make the world's books discoverable online. 

It has survived long enough for the copyright to expire and the book to enter the public domain. A public domain book is one that was never subject 
to copyright or whose legal copyright term has expired. Whether a book is in the public domain may vary country to country. Public domain books 
are our gateways to the past, representing a wealth of history, culture and knowledge that's often difficult to discover. 

Marks, notations and other marginalia present in the original volume will appear in this file - a reminder of this book's long journey from the 
publisher to a library and finally to you. 

Usage guidelines 

Google is proud to partner with libraries to digitize public domain materials and make them widely accessible. Public domain books belong to the 
public and we are merely their custodians. Nevertheless, this work is expensive, so in order to keep providing this resource, we have taken steps to 
prevent abuse by commercial parties, including placing technical restrictions on automated querying. 

We also ask that you: 

+ Make non-commercial use ofthefiles We designed Google Book Search for use by individuals, and we request that you use these files for 
personal, non-commercial purposes. 

+ Refrainfrom automated querying Do not send automated queries of any sort to Google's system: If you are conducting research on machine 
translation, optical character recognition or other areas where access to a large amount of text is helpful, please contact us. We encourage the 
use of public domain materials for these purposes and may be able to help. 

+ Maintain attribution The Google "watermark" you see on each file is essential for informing people about this project and helping them find 
additional materials through Google Book Search. Please do not remove it. 

+ Keep it legal Whatever your use, remember that you are responsible for ensuring that what you are doing is legal. Do not assume that just 
because we believe a book is in the public domain for users in the United States, that the work is also in the public domain for users in other 
countries. Whether a book is still in copyright varies from country to country, and we can't offer guidance on whether any specific use of 
any specific book is allowed. Please do not assume that a book's appearance in Google Book Search means it can be used in any manner 
anywhere in the world. Copyright infringement liability can be quite severe. 

About Google Book Search 

Google's mission is to organize the world's information and to make it universally accessible and useful. Google Book Search helps readers 
discover the world's books while helping authors and publishers reach new audiences. You can search through the full text of this book on the web

christianorum nouum nomen benediceretur in terris. 

XXIII. Quod ad regnum caelorum magis gentes quam ludaei 
perueniant. u 

XXmi. Quod solo hoc ludaei acdpere ueniam possint delictorum 
suorum, si sanguinem Christi ocdsi baptismo eius ab- 
luerint et in ecclesiam transeuntes praeceptis eius obtem- 
perauerint.

nem Dei. quodsi ille orabat qui sine peccato erat, quanto ma-

uigilans continuis precibus orabat, quanto nos magis in frequen- 
tanda oratione debemus nocte uigilare!

8S Orantes autem non infructuosis nec nudis precibus ad Deum 
ueniant. inefficax petitio est, cum precatur Deiim sterilis oratio. 
nam cum omnis arbor non faciens fructum excidatur et in ignem 
mittatur, utique et sermo non habens fructum promereri Deumu 
non potest, quia nulla operatione fecundus est. et ideo scriptura 
diuina instruit dicens: bona est oratio cum ieiunio et 
eleemosyna. nam qui in die iudicii praemium pro operibus et 
eleemosynis redditurus est hodie quoque ad orationem cum opera- 
tione uenienti beniguus auditor est. sic denique Comelius cen-so 
turio cum oraret, meruit audiri. fuit faciens multas eleemosynas 
in plebem et semper orans Deum. huic circa horam nonam oranti 
adstitit angelus testimonium reddens sui operis et dicens: Gor- 
neli, orationes tuae et eleemosynae tuae ascende- 
runt ad memoriam coram Deo. ss

sed tristitia uestra in laetitiam neniet. quisnontri- 
stitia carere optet, quis non ad laetitiam uenire festinet? quando 
autem in laetitiam ueniat nostra tristitia Dominus denuo ipse 
declarat dicens: iterum uidebo uos, et gaudebit cor 
uestrum et gaudium uestrum nemo auferet a uobis. » 
cum ergo Christum uidere gaudere sit nec possit esse gaudium 
nostrum nisi quis uiderit Christum, quae caecitas animi quaeue 
dementia est amare pressuras et poenas et lacrimas mundi et 
non festinare potius ad gaudium quod numquam possit auferri. 

6 Hoc autem fit, fratres dilectissimi , quia fides deest, quiaio 
nemo credit esse uera quae promittit Deus qui uerax est, cuius 
sermo credentibus aetemus et firmus est. si tibi uir grauis et lau- 
dabilis aliquid polliceretur , haberes pollicenti fidem nec te falli 
aut decipi ab eo crederes quem stare in sermonibus adque in 
actibus suis scires. Deus tecum loquitur, et tu mente incredula» 
perfidusfluctuaris? Deus de hoc mundo recedenti inmortalitatem 
adque aetemitatem pollicetur, et dubitas? hoc est Deum omnino 
non nosse, hoc est Christum credendi magistrum peccato incre- 
dulitatis oifendere, hoc est in ecclesia constitutum fidem in domo 
fidei non habere. so

AD DEMETEIANVM.

sacrilego et uerbis impiis obstrepentem frequenter, Demetriane,

care. nec hoc sine magisterii diuini auctoritate faciebam, cum

cere, ne quando audierit inrideat sensatos sermo- 
nes tuos, et iterum: noli respondere inprudenti ad 
loinprudentiam eius ne similis fias illi, etsanctumquo- 
que iubeamur intra conscientiam nostram tenere nec inculcandum 
pords et canibus exponere loquente Domino et dicente: ne de- 
deritis sanctum canibusneque miseritis margaritas 
uestras ante porcos, ne inculcent eas pedibus suis.

discendi uenires et clamosis uocibus personans malles tua inpu-

congredi tecum , quando facilius esset et leuius turbulenti maris

S Quod autem crebrius bella continuant, quod sterilitas et fames

hoc scias esse praedictum in nouissimis temporibus multiplicari

omnibus et rogetur.

fructum salutis operantes magis pudeat, ut plus consdentiam

diuites, illos copiosis opibus afluentes, an in ecclesia praesidente

^ vum in iinnm Cartaginem conuenissent kalendis Septembribus 
episcopi plurimi ex prouincia Africa Numidia Mauritania cum 
presbyteris et diaconibuSf praesentibus etiam plebis maxima 
parte, et lectae esseut litterae lubaiani ad Cyprianum factae, 
item Cypriani ad lubaianum rescriptae de baereticis baptizandis 

loquidque postmodum Cypriano lubaianus idem rescripserit, Cy- 
prianus dixit: audistis, collegae dilectissimi, quid miki lubaianus 
coepiscopus noster scripserit consulens mediocritatem nostram 
de inlicito et profano haereticonim baptismo quidque ego ei 
jescripserim , censens scilicet quod semel adque iterum et saepe 

3S censuimus haereticos ad ecclesiam uenientes ecclesiae baptismo
